ParlaMint-ES_2022-06-30-CD220630.u3	Excuse me a moment, Mrs. Sagastizabal. Ladies and gentlemen, the plenary has begun.
ParlaMint-ES_2022-06-30-CD220630.u4	We can say that the amendments incorporated by both our group and the other groups have improved the text proposed by the Government, although I also have to say that the text could perhaps have been more ambitious in some of these respects, because it does have a great impact on the part of creation and not so much on the part of the growth phase, which is vital for companies to stay on track. With regard to lateness, some reforms are included, but little focus is on public lateness. In the area of business finance, the text has been improved through the amendments of the various groups that we have supported and which will, of course, facilitate and speed up financing measures. My group has also focused an important part of the amendments on the part of the law on the guarantee of market unity, which, according to the Government, included measures which it describes as aimed at improving the regulation and removal of obstacles to economic activities; a law with which we have never agreed and which, in fact, we resort to the Constitutional Court, giving us some reason. In this regard, we must recognize that the draft law adapts the text of the LGUM to Judgment 79/2017 of 22 June, eliminating, on the one hand, the references contained in the text to the principle of effectiveness of the actions of the competent authorities throughout the national territory-Articles 9 and 17-, content declared null and void by unconstitutional Article 6, and, on the other hand, removing the automaticity of the precautionary suspension requested by the CNMC in the course of the special administrative litigation procedure for the guarantee of market unity. We also welcome the explicit need for tax matters to be excluded from the scope of the application of this law - a matter which had caused some controversy with certain actions of the tax authorities - and this amendment is finally clarified. However, we have not just understood that certain aspects are regulated that were not necessary because they did not pose any problems and because neither has Europe required the amendment of this law. A law, the LGUM, with which the Socialist Party disagreed and which, when Esquerra requested its repeal through a PNL, voted in favour on the grounds that it was a law that had not resolved the problems of market unity in Spain and that did not respond to the territorial model of autonomy. However, you in this draft law disqualify yourselves because the draft law proposes, among other things, to extend the control measures to the various administrations by exceeding the mechanisms already provided for in the original text of the LGUM and, of course, exceeding for us the supposed inter-administrative cooperation in this matter. It thus establishes a system which, rather than cooperation, should be regarded as both a priori control and control of the actions deployed by the various public administrations, entrusting such a function to the Ministry of Economic Affairs and Digital Transformation, through the Secretariat for the Market Unit, and to the sectoral conference for the regulatory improvement of the business climate, recognizing to them the ability to enable the above-mentioned procedures for the protection of operators, which, of course, become a kind of centralized political-administrative control, alien to the supposed cooperative spirit proclaimed in the preamble to the law. For my group, the proper functioning of the relationship between the competent administrations must be ensured by means of means of collaboration; not by legal imposition or by application of schemes, at best state, hierarchical and control between the different administrations, between different institutional levels, but by making policies more effective and efficient through collaboration. In this regard, we have proposed amendments to minimize that impact. Not all have been resolved satisfactorily; however, I have to say that we have been negotiating until the last moment, and in this regard I would like to thank the Secretary of State for his efforts to bring forward positions. Finally, and in this regard, we have reached a satisfactory agreement in three amendments for which I am asking for your support today - I do not know what number the transactions are, but they are on Article 10, Article 11 and Article 28 - because they minimize some of the issues raised by my group with regard to the LGUM. That is why today, despite the fact that we have left things in the dark, although it is not the law that we would have drawn up and approved, especially in this part concerning the Law on Guarantee of the Market Unit, but we understand that it contains positive aspects to encourage the creation of enterprises and because we have managed, as I said, to minimize that impact on some of its proposals regarding the LGUM, we will abstain in the whole of the law. Nothing more and thank you very much.

From Citizens we understand support for small and medium-sized enterprises not as support for immobilism, but as support for its growth. We have to be honest with the citizens, we have to tell them the truth, and the truth is that SMEs, on average, are less productive than larger companies. This is due, among other reasons, to the fact that they do not have sufficient resources to export and innovate; on the contrary, large companies have higher economic performance, are more productive, as they pay higher wages, earn more profits and invest in the continuing training of their workers, are more competitive internationally and have a greater capacity to resist market changes and economic crises. Of course no company is obliged to grow if it does not want to, and as liberals we will defend that each company chooses its project, but also as liberals we believe that we must do everything in our power as a legislative power to remove the regulatory or market obstacles that today, ladies and gentlemen, exist and hinder the growth of companies that do want to grow and create more jobs. We have said this in public and we have repeated it in private: Citizens fully share both the spirit and the purpose of this law. That is why we regret that a bill that could have turned the Spanish business landscape in the end remains at another missed opportunity. This law suffers from a chronic problem in Spain, and it is a rule that is more intended to facilitate the work of the Administration than that in which the Administration facilitates the lives of citizens and businesses. Let's look at some examples. Example one: this law supports the regulatory improvement and business climate - it says in its explanatory statement - and establishes a procedure for the formation of companies, a procedure - it will forgive me that I would - that is born out of date, since before passing the law we know that it will be modified in the coming months by another bill. Wouldn't it be easier, ladies and gentlemen, to start this law with that new procedure and not have to wait a few months for it to change? It would be simpler and much better for businesses as well, but here the priority is to maintain the share quotas between one ministry and another. Example two: the reform of the Market Unit Guarantee Act, which this Act also refers to. It is not that it is not an ambitious reform in this respect, but it is clear that the main target of it is the Administration itself, but the Administration speaking to itself. The government is excited because they change advice for a sectoral conference and everything is more orderly within the house. Ladies and gentlemen, the rule is not to change the name of the bodies; the rule is to give effective tools to businesses and citizens so that they can exercise their rights without relying on the goodwill of the administration, because if something depends on the administrations in charge and that this administration is good, it is no longer a right of citizens and businesses, it is a favor that the administration does us. Third and last example: the elimination of thresholds, steps or regulatory leaps that make it difficult for companies to grow. In our amendments we asked for these thresholds to be changed, and we were told from the Government that they would not accept it because this really did not affect the growth of companies and, moreover, they told us that no one had asked for it in the public hearing procedure. Liberal thinking has always been prone to exalting the virtues of the market as a mechanism for allocating resources. So much so that some have come to elevate to the category of pagan god and secular what does not cease to be a tool of economic relationship between people and institutions, with their virtues and defects. That is why from the left it has always been stressed that only through efficient regulation of these mechanisms by the States can these advantages be taken advantage of. In this sense, I do not want to fail to mention the work of a Cambridge economist - brilliant student of John Maynard Keynes and teacher of teachers - Joan Robinson, in which he warned of the lack of self-regulatory capacities, as well as of the tendencies towards cartelization and oligopolization, increasingly accused in modern capitalism. In Spain this has had a very concrete translation. Here there has never, or hardly ever been, 'Manchesterian' capitalism; the major strategic sectors, the economic engines of the country have always been in the hands of a few, a few privileged and linked in a customer-like way to the State, especially from the late-offranchism, which have imposed on the rest of the sectors and economic actors their own rules. In many ways it is regrettable to see how Spain has never ceased to be the courtship of some gentlemen of composite surnames, who have sustained and multiplied fortunes by parasitizing the State and, therefore, at the expense of the development and well-being of their compatriots. Is it not a sad euphemism to classify so that they do not cease to be oligopolic capitalsaccumulated, not by the continuous effort of an entrepreneur made to himself, but by the State, that is, by the combined effort of all, who did not hesitate, however, to privatize these capitals and hand them over to a few families and to foreign capital? ParlaMint-ES_2022-06-30-CD220630.u24	Good morning. Madam President, Madam Vice-President, ladies and gentlemen, we believe that this is a good law, but like so many others, it should be subject to the improvement that would be granted by the rural guarantee mechanism, that is, passing through the rural filter, the rural proofing that we have often talked about in this House. The creation and growth of enterprises is much more difficult in the unpopulated provinces and regions, marked by the atoning economy and the demographic crisis. This law must be adapted to these conditions of rural entrepreneurs, to small enterprises which are in many cases located in the rural world of this emptied Spain and which have particular characteristics, such as their specialization in agri-food activities, in rural tourism, in the care of the elderly, in the services sector, in retail trade or in construction and renewable energy. In these provinces, in the creation of enterprises, in addition to the simplification of the institutional framework, the reduction of administrative bureaucracy and in the improvement of coordination, communication and collaboration between administrations - which this law already establishes - two additional measures are essential: on the one hand, the implementation of the processes of territorial management and environmental assessment, which are unimplemented or remain in the role, in the face of such serious issues as the massive deployment of renewables or the installation of micro-ranges; and on the other hand, proactive action by the State to stimulate the creation of innovative enterprises in dynamic sectors, on endogenous resources and human capital. In other words, there is a need for a change in the focus of extractive sectors, which take precedence over the predatory approach to opportunities in rural areas, such as disorderly renewable energy or massive livestock farming, which I have mentioned. And we say extractives because we see no commitment on the part of renewable companies, for example, neither to generate jobs in the territory nor to remove the installed wind turbines when their production cycle is over, nor to compensate the landowners. Lease contracts, for example, do not usually meet these conditions. Moving from this extractive approach to a proactive enterprise economy focused on endogenous rural resources, with a circular economy that generates jobs and products of differentiated quality, must be the objective and to this end, aid with well-directed public incentives is very effective. This would be the case, for example, of regional State aid approved by the European Commission as operating aid for companies located in areas with serious depopulation problems and which, as the Commission itself points out, could be applied in the provinces of Cuenca, Soria and Teruel, whose population density is less than 12.5 inhabitants per square kilometre. This would help SMEs, small and medium-sized enterprises. Alongside the structural measures of this law, which we value as appropriate incentives, it would also be necessary to promote measures of a territorial and sectoral nature in order to give specific attention to the enterprises of this rural environment in the unpopulated areas, since this is where it is most needed and where it is most difficult. For example, we believe that small businesses should be helped in digitizing their business and also in rules derived from the law, such as the application of the electronic invoice. By providing some data, only 35% of small business and self-employed enterprises use e-invoices in Spain, and this percentage is reduced to just 4% in the primary sector. Therefore, if the electronic invoice is implemented, these companies need the Administration itself to help them carry out these procedures by creating portals or computer tools to facilitate these procedures in a simple way. In other cases, the situation is even worse because companies do not have access to the Internet, as the Internet does not reach many empty areas of Spain. [[The Lord]] They will hardly be able to complete electronic invoices that do not have access to the Internet, and that is another competence of their ministry. The same applies to the allocation of subsidies, and we have reflected this in our amendments. Small businesses often suffer financial imbalances as a result of delays in payments by large companies or by the public administration itself. When this happens, the little financial muscle of small businesses makes them incur delays in their payments, which puts them in a situation of lateness, and this is derived from the first default that they themselves are suffering and from the lack of capacity to deal with this problem. For this reason, we believe that the invitations to tender should not be excluded for small businesses that are in a situation of lateness, but rather that we should look at where this lateness comes from. If that debt is motivated by the fact that the company is a creditor of other companies or the public administration, it should not be excluded from the calls for subsidies; precisely, it should pursue financial incompetence and reward those companies that are bearing the financial incompetence of others. Similarly, it would be necessary to establish other territorial and sectoral aid for the rural environment in Spain which has been emptied, by providing incentives and support for sectors which are at the heart of economic activity and job creation in this rural environment. For example, it would be necessary to promote a plan for the rehabilitation of housing and energy efficiency linking the PRE 5000 aid with housing rehabilitation aid and the creation of rented housing. A plan to extend designations of origin and protected geographical indications could also be promoted to highlight high-quality products that are marketed without distinction. In addition, and generally for the rural environment, to accompany these incentives with the public action of fiscal measures to encourage the rehabilitation of housing in the rural environment, with deductions from the IRPF for purchase, rent or rehabilitation, so as to boost the activity, facilitate the rehabilitation and maintenance of the residential park, which is a limiting factor for repopulation. Similarly, the reduction of the tax on transmissions for the purchase of housing in rural areas, the reduction of VAT in works of rehabilitation and energy efficiency or support services and forestry exploitation, such as the implementation of the forest cent. In emptied Spain, despite the difficulties, there is also entrepreneurship, but it needs specific and intelligent public support. Spain cannot waste entrepreneurial talent, resources or 70% of the territory, and we therefore call on the Government to commit itself to the SMEs of the rural world. Do not allow the project of rural entrepreneurs to be lost because the State has not invested enough. You will know that phrase that says that perhaps the cure of cancer is trapped in the mind of a child who cannot pay for his or her education, a phrase that is usually used to exemplify that access to education must be free, but we can also think that perhaps the next big business project is trapped in the mind of some entrepreneur who cannot grow his or her business because he or she does not have the Internet or transportation infrastructure in his or her people. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-ES_2022-06-30-CD220630.u31	Thank you very much, President. Ladies and gentlemen, on many occasions the limitations of existing insolvency systems have been pointed out. In other words, the slow and scarce use of the competitive instruments, the low utilization of the tender compared to the countries in our environment, the majority completion of these winding-up processes, often dragging the same creditors into insolvency chains, and, among many other limitations, the reduced use of the second opportunity. In this context, the transposition of Directive 19/1023 is seen as an opportunity to have rapid and effective competition procedures to ensure that insolvent debtors have access to the exemption from their debts, aligning with the harmonisation of Union requirements in order to ensure the smooth functioning of the internal market and also, on one occasion, to protect employment and the maintenance of business, by articulating preventive restructuring frameworks to avoid insolvency and ensure the viability of businesses. Ladies and gentlemen, the draft laws that we are discussing deal with these issues with regulatory changes to adapt the division of jurisdiction in the jurisdictional sphere in order to facilitate the functional application of both the directive and the consolidated text of the Competition Law, downloading to the courts of the Mercantile of some of their current competences in favor of matters considered more relevant, such as the knowledge of the competition of creditors, of natural persons who are not commercial subjects, as well as the right, no longer only the benefit, of the exemption of the unsatisfied liability so that they are judges and judges specialists who know these issues, as well as the process of specialization of the first and second instance of the commercial specialists of civil jurisdiction, amendments that we share. Alongside this, and in order to increase the effectiveness of the procedure, the competition procedure is reviewed to speed up and make it more effective, although perhaps with an excess of voluntarism it is established its maximum duration in twelve months from the opening of the first phase to the closing of the fifth. Ladies and gentlemen, the average duration of 4.5 years is inexhaustible, but the new maximum duration will depend, as the Council of State points out, on the workload of the commercial courts, on the work of the competition administration, on the electronic means employed and on the functioning of the system in general. The same can be said of the provision for appeals to provincial hearings to be resolved within two months. In this same procedural area, one of the most relevant developments is the establishment of the special procedure for micro-enterprises in order to achieve in line with the directive a greater speed and efficiency of the competition procedure, in addition to ensuring, in particular for self-employed and small entrepreneurs, that they stop going through alternative procedures such as private debt negotiation, overcoming their reluctance to compete because of their current slowness due to the current difficulty of exonerating the debts of natural persons. To this end, Book III deals with a new procedure with less costs based on official forms, with a double itinerary and no face-to-face, since all acts will be carried out electronically, a procedure in which, following the acceptance of numerous amendments, including those of our parliamentary group, its scope has been reduced in line with the novelty of the new one and the postulation has been recovered, because its exclusion raises reasonable doubt as to the right of defence and effective judicial protection, and its entry into force has been postponed, since the effectiveness of the new procedure requires, in advance, the availability of forms, the operation of electronic services and the approval of the implementation guidelines, as well as the implementation of the settlement platform, which can certainly be complex. Without forgetting, for the proper functioning of the system, the need to approve the rules of competition administration in order to clarify the functions of the various figures that can converge in these procedures, such as mediators, experts and the same administrators, which must be remembered that it drags a normative slope since 2014 to verify the professional fitness for the access to the activity, their appointment and their remuneration, in order to be able to overcome the transitional validity of the rules contained in the Competition Law in its drafting to the entry into force of Law 17/2014, and without prejudice to the fact that last-minute amendments to articles 61 and 67.4 of the draft law have clarified some important issues. Ladies and gentlemen, another milestone in this reform is the most effective configuration of the second opportunity procedure to exempt natural persons who have acted in good faith once the liquidation phase has been opened, where it has not been possible to approve or comply with the agreement or, where appropriate, the continuity plan, and in order to allow them to reintegrate into economic activity, along the lines of the classic principle of universal patrimonial responsibility contained in 1911 of the Civil Code. In order to achieve this, the extension of the exceptions contained in article 487 had to be limited during the parliamentary process so that certain debtors could have access to the exemption from their unsatisfied liabilities when they had been criminally convicted or punished administratively. The same applies to the initial claim of the project to prevent the exemption from public credit. This has also been corrected in the parliamentary process. Ladies and gentlemen, public credit, both against state and foreign finances and against the General Treasury, will no longer be an insurmountable obstacle to the effectiveness of the second opportunity, since it will benefit in full more than 90% of natural persons, since, according to data from the General Council of the Judiciary, referred to in the year 2021, from the competitive processes presented by those natural persons, only 7% have debts greater than EUR 5000 with the State Tax Treasury. And if we are talking about the self-employed, who were 3512 compared to 10,000 of the other natural persons, 75% did not have debts above 5000 euros, their average debt being 14 000. I would like to remind you that exempt public appropriations, following the amendments, can reach EUR 10,000 with state and non-state finances, and the same amount and under the same conditions with the General Treasury of Social Security. The failure to link public claims to restructuring plans, continuation plans or the same opening of negotiations has also been corrected, limiting the execution of assets and rights necessary for the continuity of the debtor's business or professional activity arising from public law claims. It has also been established that the approval and approval of the continuation plans will not require approval, provided that the withdrawals of ordinary credits under public law do not exceed 15%. Book II, for its part, deals with a complete transformation of the pre-competitions with a single procedure: restructuring plans, establishing for them a regulatory framework for completing the negotiations and protecting the interests at stake and with limited judicial intervention, so that debtors can detect their difficulties as soon as possible and take appropriate action, because the sooner they take them, the more likely they will be to avoid insolvency. In this connection, part of the legislation in force has also been amended in order to allow for the application of deferrals and instalments of credits with the State Treasury in a voluntary or executive period, without the need for guarantees, up to a limit of €30,000. Finally, Mr President, I welcome the intensive work of all the parliamentary groups to improve the draft - also that of the ministerial representatives and the services of the House - and regret the short time that we have had, since the deadline for transposition of the directive was urgent and the milestone at the end of the month linked to the recovery and resilience funds also expired. A reform of this stalemate needed more calm; we will see how it works. And he pointed out two last questions: the retributive limitations to the competition administration in contests to be known, which possibly retract the most skilled...

ParlaMint-ES_2022-06-30-CD220630.u37	Thank you very much, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, there are two initiatives at this stage, the proposal for a law amending in part the powers or scope of the Commercial Court and the reform of the Competition Law. Firstly, with regard to the draft law amending the powers of commercial courts, this is undoubtedly a consequence of the adaptation to the State legislation of Directive 2019/2013, which has a side effect in the reform of the Competition Law, which we will then address. In fact, this bet on the specialization of the commercial courts we see well, while we will defend it also when the draft law on organizational efficiency is processed for other courts. In this area, they are aware that the time limit under the Children's Act has already expired for specialized juvenile, family and capacity support courts. Therefore, this line of work in the specialization of courts, as has been done with the Commercial Court, we understand that it also has to be a line of work in other areas of justice to improve service, to improve ultimately the resolution of cases in the judicial field. The truth is that here there was the temptation to make only one transposition of the directive, and we saw it not only as the duty to transpose the directive, but as an opportunity to address three issues that were strategic for us: one, the maintenance of legal aid; two, the question of SMEs and the special procedure, and, three, the question of public credit. In this area, our amendments and the compromises and transactions that we have come to support support the mandatory interception of defence counsel in procedures that we understand to be complex and with very relevant consequences, for which it was not logical that the project should come out without this legal assistance in the processing of these competition projects, as well as the definition, characteristics and profile of the competition administrators. We understood that it was necessary for those professionals who would accompany the persons subject to the competition procedure to be qualified professionals in the field. The SME issue, a procedure that was called special but ended up affecting virtually all the companies that went through a competition. We have managed to reduce the scope of the special SME procedure so that it is indeed a special procedure for SMEs, with levels of turnover and levels of the number of workers who comply with the reality of a special procedure. Finally, there is the question of public credit, which was incomprehensiblely overprotected in the bill. Today we are convinced that this access to the second chance is going to be possible for more than 90% of natural persons, even for more than 80% of natural persons who carry out some kind of economic activity, increasing those discounts to which the Administration -1000 euros of Social Security and 1000 euros of Finance - rising up to 20,000 euros -10 000 euros of Social Security and 10 000 euros of Finance -, with the corresponding sections of that item of 5000 and 5000. We understand that there was certainly an approach here to transposing the directive, but real work has been done and the opportunity has been taken to improve the content of the competition legislation and the competition processes. That is not all, but we understand that it contemplates necessary progress, significant progress and that they can improve, which is ultimately what moves us, the help to people, businesses and SMEs who suffer from the situation of going through a competitive procedure. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-ES_2022-06-30-CD220630.u51	President, dear colleagues, indeed, it is a law of second chance, and the second chance is an idea that is not only applicable to procedural justice, but is applicable to justice in general and is even a way of understanding the world. Faced with the idea that the world is based on a cruel competition, in which only the best win, that it is composed of winners and losers and that by a law of meritocracy and by a law whose existence no one has demonstrated, that it is that of the invisible hand, each one has what it deserves, Faced with this there is an idea that understands that the human being and the enterprises that the human being undertakes go through moments of difficulty, moments of uncertainty, in which they need the help of the community and of the institutions to have a second chance in life. It is a way of understanding the world that underlies this law. We had a competition law that was not particularly bad, but it was a slow and inefficient competition law, as is most of our justice, and that is why this Government, the Ministry of Justice in particular, is engaged in a package of measures to make justice faster, faster, more efficient, measures that include this law, in addition to the law of organizational efficiency, procedural efficiency, etc. It's an absolutely necessary package. To this whole normative package, what has our parliamentary group tried to contribute? We have submitted to this Competition Law a total of fifty-four amendments which have tried to introduce technical improvements, improvements in the functioning of the competition mechanism, but above all something fundamental of what was lacking in the original project, which is social content, that is, a different way of understanding the company which is much more in line with what the Constitution says and with what is a social and democratic state of law, which is not a liberal state. A social and democratic state of law understands that the enterprise is not only the entrepreneur, but that it is the two factors that make up it, the entrepreneur and the workers. That is why we have tabled a number of amendments that seek to give a social meaning to this law and to understand that the company is all that set that must be rescued. We have tabled some amendments - I would like to pay particular attention to transactions 4 and 5 - in which we are trying to stop all the small businesses that make up the best of our productive fabric, companies that have three or four employees and in which the company's owner, in addition to having a transitional liquidity problem, often also has a generational relief problem. There are thousands of companies that are lost each year and it is important that they stop getting lost when their employees can assume responsibility for them. We have tabled two amendments so that when workers want to turn them into social economy enterprises, cooperative enterprises, labour societies, they can do so. We tell the judge to give preferential attention to that proposal that comes from the company's own workers to save, to rescue the business project from third people and to allow them to capitalize on what they have contributed to unemployment, because it is of justice. [[Applauds]] So we will be able to save thousands of businesses in this country and we will make the constitutional mandate of Article 129.2 be implemented, that companies be saved and that conventional companies are committed to the social economy, which is the greatest and most beautiful thing this country has. The great American scientist Lynn Margulis, a person little known - perhaps because that scientist was a woman - disassembled the entire Darwinian paradigm, the whole idea of social Darwinism, the whole idea of human beings progressing in a hypercompetitive way and leaving each other behind, surviving only the strongest. Lynn Margulis disassembled that paradigm and demonstrated that simple competition without cooperation does not allow for progress, that the progress of societies, the progress of plant species, of animal species and above all of the human being is due more to cooperation, to mutual support than to competition. If the President allows me, I will make a request to all the groups of this Congress and also to the Government of Spain to transmit to this youth that we have in this country, which is a real luxury - we have a better prepared youth than ever in history - the idea that the economy is not only progressing for individual initiative, for the particular interest, and that the general interest should be abandoned to an invisible hand that no one has shown it exists; that people can co-ordinate and match their particular benefit, their particular interest with the general interest, with the common good of society, and that this is how societies progress, as Margulis has already shown. This is something they have not been taught in their education system, because they have neither been taught who Lynn Margulis was, nor have they been taught what article 129 of the Constitution says. If such a well-prepared young man knew these things, he would probably be much more responsible for the future of his country. ParlaMint-ES_2022-06-30-CD220630.u57	Thank you, President. Ladies and gentlemen, one of the main functions of the Administration of Justice is to resolve disputes before the courts. Today we bring to this Chamber the adoption of an organic bill that pursues two fundamental keys: speeding up and achieving efficiency in the handling and resolution of cases. It is a sister law of the Competition Law, which balances the division of powers of the courts of the Mercantile and the specialized sections of the provincial hearings and, correlatively, that of the other sections and courts of first instance; a law which acts as a solution to the great increase in cases, which have increased exponentially. I'll give you an example of what's happening in the summer season. Who has never had an incident with the airlines or knows any family member or friend who has. There are thousands of lawsuits accumulated for loss of suitcases, for delays, for cancellations, many still not admitted. Following the implementation of the mediation procedure, voluntary and non-binding for the passenger and binding and compulsory for the airlines, this law brings as a novelty that its competence passes to the courts of first instance, a solution that responds to the demand of the judicial sector. Ladies and gentlemen, it is urgent to find the right road map to reduce the length of the cases of the Mercantile courts, which is the most collapsed body, with proceedings lasting almost fifty-five months, more than four years on average. The number of cases admitted from 2015 to 2019 was reduced by 26%, but the number of cases resolved was reduced by only 29.8%. Here is the first key: agility in the handling and resolution of cases. Our laws must adapt to changes and respond to citizens' demands, so it is essential to seek solutions. Ladies and gentlemen of the People's Party, your proposals are purely incremental. More tried, they say. Read the law, read it, because it already envisages the creation of new courts or the extension of its jurisdiction to other provinces. [[Applauds]] But it's not just about quantity, it's about quality. Look, ladies and gentlemen, we could not raise this parallel law with the competition without talking about the competitions of non-entrepreneurial natural persons in situations of personal economic crisis, cases that have reached in number that of entrepreneurial legal persons, becoming one of the main sources of work for judges. What's the problem here? The award of these competitions, under popular government, to the courts of first instance, a completely wrong decision because it lacks the necessary knowledge to resolve. And what was the result? A two-speed justice, in the processing of the competition and in the matter of unpaid debts, and the courts of the Mercantile experts in the field will be those who recover the competition. Ladies and gentlemen, this government is committed to quality laws, adapted to the European framework, laws that protect the bad economic situations of citizens, and are an oxygen balloon for businesses. Here, the second key, the effectiveness of case handling and resolution, something that will come in hand with procedural and digital organizational efficiency laws. Moreover, this law, although it may seem brief, will be fundamental support of the Competition Law. I would like to thank the contributions of civil society and legal actors. I would also like to thank our advisers from the parliamentary group, the groups in this House who have facilitated the way for us and my dear Paco Aranda for always helping me. [[Applauds]] It is essential to transform our public service to make it more agile, efficient and quality. This must be the way to widen a bottleneck that still drowns our justice. As the former jurists said, ad justitiam per ius, to justice for the law. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2022-06-30-CD220630.u163	... that small and medium-sized enterprises are vital to the Spanish economy, accounting for 99.8% of the business fabric, and also accounting for 66.4% of employment. I would like to point out, from this perspective, the vulnerability of these small and medium-sized enterprises to situations of economic crisis and to highlight the measures of protection towards SMEs that have been addressed by the United States during this parliamentary term, through the social shield, which contrast with the neoliberal policies that were applied by successive governments, both of the Socialist Party and of the People's Party, in the previous economic crisis, resulting from the bankruptcy of Lehman Brothers, which entailed millions of layoffs and also the closure of thousands and thousands of businesses. In the previous economic crisis, the neoliberal exit resulted in a significant loss of our productive fabric as a result of adjustment measures. I remember the adjustment decree of May 2010, which lowered the salaries of public workers; the labour reforms of 2010 and 2012, which led to the precariousness of employment, to facilitate dismissal, and which also involved the convening of three general strikes: one against the 2010 labour reform and two against the 2012 labour reform. The reforms of the public pension system in 2011 and 2013, which increased the retirement age, which reduced the purchasing capacity of pensioners through the revaluation rate introduced by the People's Party, or the adjustments and cuts in public services, such as education, health or dependency, which made employment levels in 2010 not recover until 2017 and which obviously led to a breakdown in the Spanish productive fabric. In contrast to these neoliberal policies, with the entry of the United Nations We can in the government in this legislature and in the face of the economic and social crisis resulting from the pandemic, from the health crisis that caused the gross domestic product to fall by 10.8% compared to the 3.8% that was the maximum fall of the previous economic crisis, a social shield was articulated for protection of work, protection of the family and protection, therefore also for small and medium-sized enterprises, such as the ERTEs have been mainly within the social shield, which have mobilized billions of public money and which have gone from being a mechanism of job extinction to a mechanism of employment protection that has protected 3.6 million workers, along with aid for the cessation of activity to self-employed workers, who have protected one and a half million workers. These two mechanisms of public intervention in the economy and in the productive model have saved millions of jobs and hundreds of thousands of small and medium-sized enterprises, further facilitating the early recovery of employment. We currently have more than 20 million people affiliated with Social Security, which is a record, and unemployment levels are the lowest, no longer just before the pandemic, but for the past 15 years, we are at unemployment levels in 2007. Protection measures therefore result in employment recovering for the first time above the economy, which is still more than 5 points below the pre-pandemic level. The minimum wage has been raised to EUR 1000 since 735, more than 35% of the increase, affecting millions of workers. We have always been told by neo-liberal economists that raising the minimum wage was detrimental to the economy, that it was not going to be hired, and yet we have seen how with raising the minimum wage recruitment and raising the minimum wage small and medium-sized enterprises have benefited from the greater purchasing capacity of the social majority, which we are workers. Adjustment and cut-off policies have been hampered, the budgets for the years 2021 and 2022 are the most expansive and the most social in history, and the purchasing power of pensions has been restored by repealing the pension revaluation rate, which caused pensions to rise by 0.25 % and not to rise prices. Therefore, a series of expansionary policies, social policies and protection policies for workers, families and small and medium-sized enterprises, which have saved hundreds of thousands of SMEs and enabled them to have a record number of jobs despite the profound economic crisis resulting from the pandemic. All these policies are an example of the commitment of our parliamentary group, of Unidad Podemos, of our political space, also of the Government, to the productive sectors, especially to workers, but also to small and medium-sized enterprises. I would also like to highlight the work being done since the 2030 Agenda to ensure that small and medium-sized enterprises and small municipalities have access to PERTE. It is therefore clear the commitment of our political force, our political space, to small and medium-sized enterprises. We understand that this law is positive because it increases transparency in public procurement by regulating the publication of statistics on the percentage of participation of public contracts allocated to SMEs; it is positive because it increases transparency in public procurement, makes it possible to make diagnoses for the design of public policies and their evaluation and can also develop that smaller companies in our country can have greater participation in the provision of State goods and services. But we also want to point out the limits of this initiative, since there are no clauses for the public procurement of small and medium-sized enterprises or impulse measures, and we understand that if we really want to - we appeal to the proposing group of the proposed law -...

ParlaMint-ES_2022-06-30-CD220630.u188	Thank you, President. Good morning, ladies and gentlemen. Debate for its consideration on 16 February 2021, a year and a half later, following a process of negotiation of deep work between the different parliamentary groups and a genuine deliberative exercise in which this Member has personally learned a great deal, today we finally voted the comprehensive law on equal treatment and non-discrimination, a law that transposes into the legal system of the Spanish State a European regulatory system on non-discrimination that had not yet reached a legal conclusion in our country. I would like to highlight in this respect article 14 of the European Convention on Human Rights, which corresponds precisely to article 14 of our Constitution, which guarantees the constitutional principle of equal treatment and non-discrimination, which determines the interpretation not only of fundamental rights, but of each and every one of the constitutional rights. At European level, the directives on equality in employment were adopted in 2000 which prohibited discrimination on grounds of sexual orientation, religious beliefs, age and disability in the area of employment - but not everything is employment; from the capitalist anthropological conception it is said: without work there is no dignity; but dignity I must say that it is prior to work -; in that same year the well-known directive 2000/43 on racial equality was also adopted; and in 2004, the directive on equal treatment between men and women. And in the Spanish sphere I have to highlight as a precedent the well-known law on equal treatment between men and women of 2007, which already offered an integral conception of what equal treatment means not only - I insist - at work, but in all areas that affect citizens' lives. The Universal Declaration of Human Rights states in its first article - and I must stress this - that 'all human beings are born free and equal in dignity and rights and, endowed with reason and conscience, must behave fraternally with one another'. Why am I saying this? Because although these three principles - equality, freedom and dignity - seem settled, we still have much left to make them effective in contemporary societies. And of them all, let me tell you that the principle of equality is the most important. Without equality there is no freedom, without equality there is no dignity. Not only in any legal-political community, but in any human community the principle of equality is the most basic. When one enters a community of human beings, the first thing he demands is: treat me just like the rest or, otherwise said, do not discriminate against me. However, as the Republican Group's partner pointed out, we have recently seen a comparative aggravation when we saw how human rights were violated, precisely because of the violation of the principle of equality and non-discrimination, at the border of Melilla. We have seen how people, who are ultimately equal in dignity, simply because they have a different national origin, have been treated differently when seeking asylum and refuge. Without equal treatment, as I said, there is no real respect for the equality of any human being, as Mrs Giménez also pointed out with regard to employment. The principle of equality goes beyond even economic equality. Those of us who work in the field of anti-discrimination law know that people with different social scales are affected when they want to enter a bar, when they want to enter a flat, when they want to enter another country, no matter how much money they actually have, simply because they have a different religion, a different culture or a different ethnicity, a different sexual orientation. That is why today I want to put this law into practice. This law is a step forward, but there are things to be done. Today we have to celebrate, but we also have to demand. As the Jewish philosopher Levinas said - and, by the way, the old friend Trotsky - the demand for democracy is permanent. The demand for justice is permanent. We cannot settle for the adoption of this law as it stands. Obviously, it is a satisfactory law, but we must continue to make progress in reforming the status of the victim, in reforming the regulation of emigrants, in reforming the Aliens Act. You have to keep fighting and never give up. I would also like to emphasise - for me in particular and for my parliamentary group - that the thrust of the Sub-Commission on a State Pact against Anti-Gypsyism has had a special significance and, in some way, time has wanted to run in parallel with this equal treatment and non-discrimination law. I would therefore like to point out that, thanks to an impetus for debate in the period of the motion - the amendments, as has been mentioned here, of the Citizens' Group and of the United Nations We can be accepted in a compromise by the Socialist Group and voted in favour of all the parliamentary groups, to which I am grateful, except VOX, who abstained - the Penal Code was amended to include anti-Gyptanism as a hate crime in 510 and also in 22.4 as an aggravating offence. This Member simply wishes to thank us for the moment we have been living with the adoption of this law, which many of us have hoped for since 2000 at European level, and to remind you that democracy is a permanent, constant exercise and that we can never be completely satisfied with what we have adopted in this House. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]
